## A.2.1 RecZero (�<sub>�</sub>): Slow-Thinking Structured Reasoning.

Input: User history with previously rated books and   
reviews, followed by the target item:   
Target item: Shifting Fates (Urban Fantasy Shifter   
Romance); Rating: ?   
Output:   
<analyze\_user> The user prefers emotionally engaging   
romance, morally complex characters, strong   
world-building, and unconventional romantic settings.   
</analyze\_user>   
<analyze\_item> The target item is an urban fantasy   
shifter romance with a protective female lead and   
survival-focused premise.   
</analyze\_item>   
<match> The target item matches the user’s preference   
for strong romantic tension, fantasy settings, and   
distinctive world-building, although the multi-author   
format may introduce some uncertainty.   
</match>   
<rate> 4.0 </rate>   
RecZero generates a four-segment output containing a three-segment   
reasoning trace before producing the final rating.

## A.2.2 TALLRec (�<sub>�</sub>): Fast-Thinking Direct Prediction.

Instruction: Predict rating for the last item. Input: Same user history and target item as above. Output: 4.0/5.0 TALLRec predicts the rating directly without generating intermediate reasoning, producing only a short numerical response.

D<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ithf<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ness uses</sub> t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on mass, no</sub>t <sub>per-</sub>t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> d<sub>en-</sub> <sub>s</sub>it<sub>y,</sub> b<sub>ecause</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l i<sub>s mean</sub>t t<sub>o measure</sub> h<sub>ow muc</sub>h <sub>m</sub>atch evidence enters the decision-time re<sub>p</sub>resentation<sub>,</sub> not how much attention match receives <sub>p</sub>er token. B.3.1 Length-normalised variants. Our main definition uses total <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on mass</sub> b<sub>ecause</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ithf<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ness</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o measure</sub> evidence contribution to the decision re<sub>p</sub>resentation. Since <sub>prompt,</sub> a<sub>n</sub>a<sub>lyze,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>m</sub>a<sub>tch</sub> dif<sub>er</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub> l<sub>eng</sub>th<sub>,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> thi<sub>s a</sub>lt<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y.</sub>

## B.4.2 From Hessian to a Diagonal Empirical-Fisher Surrogate.

Hessian decomposition. $$

Since t<sup>h</sup>e o<sup>b</sup>jective is <sup>l</sup>inear an<sup>d</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>easi<sup>bl</sup>e set is convex w<sup>h</sup>en $s _ { b } \geq 0 ,$ the KKT conditions are suficient for <sub>g</sub>lobal o<sub>p</sub>timalit<sub>y</sub>.

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>coe</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\alpha _ { b }$ <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>�,</sub> <sub>so</sub> th<sub>ey</sub> <sub>canno</sub>t b<sub>e c</sub>h<sub>osen</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce a mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>li<sub>er</sub> $\mu \geq 0$

f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>;</sub> f<sub>or</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d $\mu ,$ th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pro</sub>bl<sub>em</sub> <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>across</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s, an</sub>d $\mu$ is <sup>l</sup>ater a<sup>d</sup>juste<sup>d</sup> unti<sup>l</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e resu<sup>l</sup>ting coe<sup>fi</sup>cients satis<sup>f</sup>y t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>b</sup>u<sup>d</sup>get con<sup>d</sup>ition. T<sup>h</sup>e Lagrangian o<sup>b</sup>jective is

$$
\sum _ { b \in \mathcal { B } } \left( w _ { b } \alpha _ { b } - \mu s _ { b } \alpha _ { b } ^ { 2 } \right) + \mu \varepsilon .
$$

Th<sub>us eac</sub>h <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e so</sub>l<sub>ves</sub>

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { 0 \leq \alpha _ { b } \leq \bar { \alpha } } \ w _ { b } \alpha _ { b } - \mu s _ { b } \alpha _ { b } ^ { 2 } .
$$

F<sub>o</sub>r $s _ { b } > 0$ <sub>an</sub>d $\mu > 0 ;$ <sub>,</sub> the unconstrained stationar<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>oint satisfies

$$
\frac { \partial } { \partial \alpha _ { b } } \left( w _ { b } \alpha _ { b } - \mu s _ { b } \alpha _ { b } ^ { 2 } \right) = w _ { b } - 2 \mu s _ { b } \alpha _ { b } = 0 , \qquad \alpha _ { b } ( \mu ) = \frac { w _ { b } } { 2 \mu s _ { b } } .
$$

A<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> the box constraint <sub>g</sub>ives the water-fillin<sub>g</sub> solution

$$

$$

The m<sub>u</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>lie ${ } ^ { \cdot \mu }$ is not a fixed h<sub>yp</sub>er<sub>p</sub>arameter<sub>;</sub> it is solved se<sub>p</sub>aratel<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>merge.</sub> I<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bi<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ng case,</sub> $\mu > 0$ i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>oun</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> bi<sub>sec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>

$$
\sum _ { b \in \mathcal { B } } s _ { b } ( \alpha _ { b } ^ { \star } ) ^ { 2 } = \varepsilon .
$$

I<sup>f</sup> <sup>f</sup>u<sup>ll</sup> injection a<sup>l</sup>rea<sup>d</sup>y satis<sup>fi</sup>es t<sup>h</sup>e pertur<sup>b</sup>ation constraint, t<sup>h</sup>en th<sub>e</sub> <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>l<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>.</sub> C<sub>omp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>ary</sub> <sub>s</sub>l<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>ness</sub> <sub>se</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\mu = 0 ,$ <sub>, an</sub>d the o<sub>p</sub>timal solution is $\alpha _ { b } ^ { \star } = \bar { \alpha }$ f<sub>or a</sub>ll bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s.</sub>

Wh<sub>en</sub> $s _ { b } = 0 ;$ bl<sub>oc</sub>k � <sub>carr</sub>i<sub>es</sub> <sub>no</sub> <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>pena</sub>lt<sub>y.</sub> If $w _ { b } > 0$ t<sup>h</sup>e o<sup>b</sup>jective is maximise<sup>d</sup> <sup>b</sup>y setting ${ \alpha } _ { b } ^ { \star } = \bar { \alpha } ;$ if $w _ { b } = 0 ,$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ues</sub> in $[ 0 , \bar { \alpha } ]$ <sub>are equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>en</sub>t<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>we se</sub>t $\alpha _ { b } ^ { \star } = \bar { \alpha }$ b<sub>y</sub> convention.
